--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes to remove such that the two black nodes (A and I) are completely disconnected, we need to identify all the paths between these two nodes and find the set of nodes whose removal will disconnect all these paths.

By examining the graph:

- **Node A** is connected to nodes B, P, and Q.
- **Node I** is connected to nodes B, H, N, Q, and J.

Next, we identify paths from A to I:
1. A -> B -> I
2. A -> B -> N -> I
3. A -> B -> C -> T -> N -> I
4. A -> P -> Q -> I
5. A -> P -> Q -> J -> I

To completely disconnect A and I, removing the correct set of nodes must break all these paths. Analyzing the paths:

- **Removing Node B** directly breaks paths 1, 2, and 3.
- **Removing Nodes Q** would break paths 4 and 5.
- However, we need a minimal set. Checking subsets:

  - Remove Nodes B and P: This will break paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
  - There is no need to remove Q separately since removing P ensures that path 4 is cut.

Thus, the smallest set of nodes that disconnects the two black nodes is:

**Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, P]**
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 1, 'cut_set': ['H']}

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (S and R), we need to identify the paths between them and find the critical nodes whose removal will break all these paths.

1. **Identify Paths between S and R:**
   - S -> B -> G -> J -> I -> P -> Q -> O -> R
   - S -> B -> G -> J -> I -> H -> J -> O -> R
   - S -> E -> F -> T -> B -> G -> J -> O -> R
   - S -> E -> F -> T -> B -> G -> Q -> O -> R
   - S -> B -> G -> Q -> O -> R

2. **Determine Critical Nodes:**
   - Removing node **B** would break all paths involving S -> B -> (any intermediate nodes) -> R.
   - Removing node **G** would break all paths involving S -> (any intermediate nodes) -> G -> (any intermediate nodes) -> R.

### Path Analysis:
1. **Direct Path from Q to N**: There is no direct edge between Q and N.
2. **Indirect Paths**:
   - Q → B → N
   - Q → H → I → B → N
   - Q → H → N
   - Q → J → B → N
   - Q → J → I → B → N
   - Q → S → P → A → B → N  (this involves intermediate nodes)

### Cut Set Analysis:
Given the above paths, we need to find the minimum set of edges that can be removed to break all these paths.

1. **Removing Q → J**: This breaks the paths through J.
2. **Removing H → N**: This breaks the paths through H.
3. **Removing B → N**: This breaks the paths through B.

By removing these edges, all paths between Q and N are disconnected.

### Conclusion:
The minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes Q and N is 3.

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(Q, J), (H, N), (B, N)].
